Round three · 42 ideas · every one depth-audited

Nobody wrote
this tech tree

Civilizations that start with cold hands and flint and end somewhere neither of us can predict. The hard part was never the simulation — it is making sure the discoveries are real discoveries and not your own content handed back to you in a shuffled order.

42ideas
0open-ended
29ceilinged
13fake depth
Read that tally carefully. An adversarial critic attacked every idea's depth claim, and not one survived as genuinely open-ended on its own. Thirteen turned out to be authored content in disguise. That is the real finding of this round — and the reason the answer below is a specific hybrid rather than a favourite.

What I would actually build

The judge’s synthesis, drawn from three ideas in the pool. It is ordered so that the first genuine surprise arrives on a Saturday afternoon, before a single agent exists — and so the one number that decides whether the whole project is interesting gets measured on day one.

Build ONE project, in this order, and name it after its substrate rather than its story. Spine: Palimpsest's wake/sleep library learning (Stitch as the compressor, LILO-style naming) running over The Slow Kiln's GENERATED chemistry — with Glyphworks' works-as-rule-schema-instances as the depth mechanism, and three cheap mechanisms stolen from elsewhere.

Weekend 1

chemistry, no agents. Author a GENERATIVE CORE, not rules: ~40 element tokens with a seeded table of about fifty numbers (valence, mass, affinity, an electronegativity analog, an enthalpy contribution, phase thresholds). Reaction guards and product property vectors are both DERIVED from that table by fixed formulas, so nobody — including you — has a list of what can be made, and a new world is a new draw of fifty numbers rather than a rewrite. Intern substances by canonical hash, memoise every (rule, inputs, conditions) tuple, then brute-force 100k random process programs and read what came out. Do this before any agent exists; it is the fastest genuine surprise available in the whole pool and it also measures, on day one, the single number that decides whether the project is interesting: the size of the reachable closure from the feedstock set. If that number is a few hundred, tune the core before writing anything else.

Weekend 2

the engine. A typed process lisp (~28 primitives) over substances and sites, run by a deterministic VM that charges INTERPRETER STEPS and never wall-clock, so a thermally throttling laptop cannot reshape history. Bottom-up enumerative search with observational-equivalence dedup. One lethal survival predicate. Then `pip install stitch_core`, desugar your binding forms to de Bruijn lambdas first (budget a day for this — a named-binder lisp does not pipe into Stitch, whatever the pitches say), and mint the top abstraction as a new primitive. Re-run and confirm the same task now solves in a fraction of the nodes. Wire ntfy. That night your phone tells you a word you did not write.

The gates

The three gates that separate this from a junk generator, all in weekend 2: an abstraction is admitted only if it (a) clears an MDL threshold, (b) delivers amortised INTERPRETER-STEP savings across the corpus — this fixes Ashcairn's compression-is-metabolism category error, since Stitch minimises AST size and will otherwise mint short expensive abstractions your survival selection then punishes — and (c) is used by at least two bands on at least two distinct task templates before it earns a NAME. Everything else stays `unnamed fragment #331`. The naming model is the component with the least epistemic content and the most persuasive output; do not let it christen plumbing.

Weekends 3-4

the two things that make month three unpredictable. First, WORKS: rewrite the derived physics as ~6 rule schemas with open constants, and let a stationary expensive abstraction be built as a works that instantiates a schema with constants it can physically sustain. A kiln does not cache a recipe — it makes a region of the reaction space reachable that no hand-executed program can reach at any budget. That is the difference between "cheaper" and "possible", and it is the only mechanism here where the built environment extends the rule set rather than reaching further along a fixed one. Key the observational-equivalence bank by (eval vector, works-context bitset, site EPOCH) — works are built and decay, so this is a cache-coherence problem, and getting it wrong gives you wrong search results that are very hard to debug. Second, the AUTOTELIC TASK GENERATOR, which every audit in the pool independently names as the real bottleneck: a MAP-Elites archive (pyribs, v0.11.0 stable) over composite property space — hardness × toughness × ignition × conductivity — emitting "produce an object landing in an empty cell" as goals, filtered by learning progress (keep a goal unsolved at budget B and solved at 3B, retire it once cheap). Survival predicates stay the lethal floor; property-frontier cells become the receding ceiling. Without this the whole thing plateaus in six weeks; with it, the goal set grows exactly as fast as the works stack opens new regions of chemistry.

Culture

Then the culture layer, which is what makes it a world rather than a chart. Knowledge is carriers: `knows(agent, abstraction, fidelity)`, teaching is a costed program, fidelity falls with DAG depth, and when the last carrier dies the entry is deleted and every dependent recursively invalidated. Add Marginalia's scribal-error operator on transmission — dittography, haplography, numeral corruption, marginal-gloss absorption — as a second, free variation channel alongside search, since a corrupted recipe occasionally outperforms its parent and you get the stemma for nothing. Run 3-4 worlds with different element genomes and transplant abstractions nightly: an abstraction's GENERALITY SCORE (fraction of foreign chemistries where it still earns call-sites) is a computed, unauthored classification into law versus local trick, and it is the cheapest real measure of depth in this entire pool.

Novelty

Novelty, ANDed hard down to at most three pushes a day plus a guaranteed 07:00 digest: FIRST-EVER canonical hash; a new works opening a physical regime never before reached (the headline — this is "max achievable temperature" as the one gauge that says how advanced the world is); KEYSTONE (articulation point in the tech DAG, Tarjan, milliseconds); FORGETTING and REDISCOVERY (same behaviour signature on a fixed 64-state probe, different body — the one signal that cannot be fooled by a naming model); and SEEDED COUNTERFACTUAL CONSEQUENCE on the top ten adopted abstractions, matched cohorts across several seeds, reporting survival delta, so the feed reports what MATTERED rather than what was new. Run a matched null world with library learning disabled and put it as a thin strip on the phone card; if your curves move the same in both, the engine is not the cause and you want to know that in week two, not month four.

The check-in

one hero card — the new named thing, its five-line ancestry as plain text, the works glyph on a small map, and one gauge. Binge: the encyclopedia — etymology DAG, who currently knows it, what dies without it, and the graveyard of lost techniques with the name of the last person who knew each one. Local Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M (~3GB, real and released March 2026, comfortable on 8GB with a bge-small embedder resident) under GBNF for names; one nightly Claude Haiku 4.5 batch pass over the ~30 most consequential entries for the chronicle voice, which at $1/$5 per MTok with the 50% batch discount and ~0.1x cached reads is roughly $0.20 a night. The LLM never touches adjudication. The GPU's real job is the ~200k-parameter recognition MLP that keeps enumerative search from stalling at depth 3 as the library grows — that is the job every audit says is required and every pitch assigns to naming.

The payoff

What you get that no hand-authored tree gives you: month three contains a nine-step composite whose property vector nobody planned, standing in a works whose sustained regime was unreachable in month one, held by four people in one band, with a printable body and a lineage you can walk — and a number telling you whether it also works under a different periodic table.

The shortlist

Ranked for you, with the auditor’s depth verdict attached. The depth line is the one to read — it says whether the thing keeps inventing after month two, and what it costs to make it.

8.8fit

Palimpsest (chronicle lens — bands, generated materials, encyclopedia)

ChroniclesCeilinged
Depth

Real but ceilinged at the task distribution, not the compressor. Depth is genuine through ~2 months and then flat unless the curriculum is made endogenous. With an autotelic goal generator bolted on it is the strongest depth engine here; without one it is a very good two-month project.

Why

Best-integrated instance of the only mechanism in the pool where an invention is a named, executable, inspectable object with a birth certificate: Stitch (stitch_core, POPL'23, verified real and genuinely 3-4 orders faster than DreamCoder's compressor) mines abstractions out of programs that actually kept a band alive, LILO-style auto-documentation names them, and the citation DAG IS the tech tree. Crucially it does NOT author the materials — a per-world generated table of substances plus a reaction map means the semantics are unauthored too, which is what separates it from Emberlisp's hand-written ignition thresholds. The builder can literally read `bank-the-coals`, print its body, see its three ancestors, and verify nobody wrote it. First weekend is real (VM + 15 primitives + enumerative search + `pip install stitch_core` + print the top abstraction) and the Sunday-afternoon payoff — the compressor names a subroutine you never wrote and Monday's search USES it — is the most reliable delight moment in the entire pool. Encyclopedia-with-etymologies is a genuine hour-long binge; the 30s card (new word, gloss, ancestry breadcrumb, bits saved) is tight. CPU-dominant, GPU only for naming, no API bill worth naming.

Risk

The audit's corpus-homogeneity ceiling is correct and arrives ~week 8: world-derived tasks are bimodal (trivially solved or unreachable), so nightly MDL delta collapses and Stitch returns refactors of a solved corpus forever. Second: Stitch maximises compression, not meaning — roughly 3 in 4 abstractions will be positional plumbing that a 4B model will confidently christen anyway, manufacturing the appearance of depth exactly where there is least. Also literal: the PyPI package is `stitch_core`, and a stateful lisp with named binders does NOT pipe into Stitch unchanged — desugar to de Bruijn lambdas or it will treat variable names as primitives. Budget a day.

Pairs with

The Slow Kiln (library-learning lens)

8.6fit

The Slow Kiln (library-learning lens — the artificial-chemistry one)

Library learningCeilinged
Depth

Real at the process layer, shallow at the substance layer. Its own audit's fix — replace 15 authored rules and 7 property functions with a generative core of ~50 per-element numbers (affinity, valence, mass, enthalpy contribution) and derive both guards and properties from them — is cheap, converts the chemistry into a mutable genome, and is the highest value-per-line edit anywhere in this pool. Do that and it stops being an authored gradient.

Why

The single best first-weekend-to-surprise ratio in the pool, and the cleanest argument for why compressed fragments mean something. Author ~15 guarded reaction rules over composition multisets, not recipes; brute-force 100k random process programs on Saturday afternoon and you are already looking at products you did not anticipate BEFORE any agent exists. Then Stitch over the corpus of successful recipes mines the recurring fragments — and this is the one place in the whole pool where MDL and physical significance genuinely coincide, because a repeated 'hold above T then quench' really is a unit process. That is a much stronger claim than any other Stitch-based entry can make. Compute honesty is the best of all thirty ideas: recipes are PURE functions of a substance record, so observational-equivalence dedup and memoisation are cheap in the way DreamCoder's are and embodied variants' are not; interned substances plus a `(rule, inputs, conditions)` memo makes chemistry effectively free after first evaluation. Property-grid phone view (hardness × toughness, cells shaded by recency, tonight's substances as bright dots) shows progress as territory and is instantly legible. RAF closure detection is ~50 lines and gives a genuinely non-arbitrary threshold event.

Risk

Two real ones. Substances saturate into sludge — rule-based chemistries have thermodynamic attractors and ~60% of recipes will terminate in three products by week three; the used-vs-produced ratio is the honest telemetry and must ship in week one, not later. And the RAF headline is at high risk of being an authored artifact: with 15 hand-written rules a self-sustaining set is usually either present on night one or structurally impossible, decided by you when you write rule 7. A threshold event that fires once and never again is a screenshot, not a mechanism.

Pairs with

Glyphworks

8.4fit

Glyphworks

Library learningCeilinged
Depth

Highest genuine ceiling in the pool, but it grows in STOREYS and you author how many exist — ~3-5 with a fixed rule set. Its audit's one change is the real unlock and I endorse it strongly: rewrite the physics as ~6 rule SCHEMAS with open constants, and let a works instantiate a schema with constants it can physically sustain. Then the built environment extends the RULE SET rather than reaching further along a fixed one, storey count stops being authored, and the syntax→cost table deletes itself. Build it weekend 3-4 on an existing substrate, never standalone.

Why

The only design whose depth mechanism changes what is POSSIBLE rather than what is cheap, and that distinction is the whole ask. A works is a built object that installs a place-indexed primitive AND reaches a physical regime bare hands cannot — a hearth that sustains a temperature no `heat` call can hold. So each realised technology expands the boundary of what is realisable next, computed rather than enumerated: Kauffman's adjacent possible with an actual mechanism under it. Second-order works (built from a material only another works can make) is a real, detectable industry threshold. It also has the best bullshit detector of any idea in the pool: the map. A field of never-used buildings is self-evidently a bug, whereas a list of 90 named abstractions looks like success. The map-as-check-in plus tap-and-hold time-lapse of the whole valley history is the most shareable artifact here.

Risk

Hardest engineering in the pool and it is not close. Place-indexed enumeration with context-partitioned observational equivalence is a cache-coherence problem, not a partitioning problem — works are built and decay, so the context signature at a site mutates and invalidates bank entries keyed to it. Get it wrong and you either blow 64GB or silently share banks across incompatible contexts and get wrong search results that are miserable to debug. Expect the first month to be the enumerator, not the watching. Two authored seams to close: the stationarity heuristic that promotes abstractions to blueprints (you will tune it until it promotes the buildings you hoped for) and the hand-written syntax→build-cost table, which is a recipe book in disguise.

Pairs with

Palimpsest (chronicle lens)

8.1fit

Emberlisp

Library learningCeilinged
Depth

Real chunking over an enumerable effect set. Its own one-change (MAP-Elites over composite property space as the task generator, so goals grow as fast as the material space the abstractions open) is ~80 lines and converts a flat 4-10 week plateau into a receding frontier. Ship that in month one, not as a fallback.

Why

The crispest specification of the core engine — tables, primitives, budgets, gates, phone view — and the one I would hand to a solo builder as the reference implementation of wake/sleep-in-a-world. Death is real, population is search budget (surviving buys more invention, and the death spiral is watchable in the numbers), use-it-or-lose-it demotion prunes MDL junk, and the ntfy line 'The Ash clan invented SPARK-ON-DRY-MOSS (fn_3, arity 2) — compresses 14 programs, MDL -22%' is exactly the 'you wrote no such word' moment the brief is asking for. Benchmark hygiene is correct and stated: node expansions and interpreter steps, never wall-clock, so a hot laptop and a cool one produce identical history. The transfer test across valley resets (does this abstraction survive relocation — local trick or law?) is a free, computed, unauthored classification of depth.

Risk

Two, both real. The physics is 12 rules and 14 property records, so the SET of reachable qualitative effects is small enough that you could enumerate it in an afternoon — 'fire' is fully pre-figured because you wrote the ignition threshold and the percussion-sparks rule. What is genuinely emergent is the ORDER and the CHUNKING, not the menu. Second and more urgent: unlike pure DreamCoder domains, evaluating a candidate here means ROLLING OUT a stateful world, so observational-equivalence dedup is far weaker and a Python tree-walker gives you maybe low millions of nodes per core-night against a need for 10^6-10^7. 'Port to Rust only if profiling demands it' is wrong — profiling will demand it in week one.

Pairs with

The Long Teaching

7.6fit

Sandwright

Combinatorial inventionCeilinged
Depth

Authentic but kind-bounded. Genuine unauthored emergence for 6-10 weeks, then MAP-Elites fills cells with mechanically valid near-duplicates while archive coverage keeps rising — progress that photographs beautifully and means nothing. Budget for redesigning descriptors twice, and treat 'add one material rule' as the scheduled, measurable, actually-fun maintenance ritual it is.

Why

The best answer in the pool to semantic risk: no LLM anywhere in the adjudication loop, so nothing can be invented that does not physically work, because working is defined by simulation. The oxygen-starved charcoal pile really is a consequence of rules you wrote that you did not anticipate — you authored a GENERATOR, not a catalogue, which is precisely the distinction the brief demands. And it has by a distance the best watchability of anything here: a three-second looping WebP of the new machine actually running, autoplaying on the card. Scrolling a catalogue of little machines your laptop invented is a genuinely great evening, and nothing explains a machine faster than watching it. Receipt-printer blueprint is the right physical artifact.

Risk

Three, and one is hidden where the depth is supposed to be. (1) Falling-sand CA is NOT embarrassingly parallel — movement has write conflicts and update-order dependence, so a GPU port needs Margolus/block partitioning or atomics, which CHANGES the dynamics; the recommended 'debug on CPU then port' plan will shift the fitness landscape under you and may invalidate every archive elite. Pin determinism early. (2) MODULE INSERTION is much weaker than advertised: CA sub-assemblies couple non-locally through shared heat/pressure/flow fields, so measured transfer functions do not compose, and the 'physics surprise' detector (>3σ from linear composition of parts) will fire constantly because non-additivity is the default. (3) The 2D CA cannot represent rigid bodies, rotation or sealed pistons, so the month 3-6 ladder (heat engines, trip hammers) is unreachable; what it actually reaches is the thermal/fluid/chemical family, ~15-30 machine classes.

Pairs with

Glyphworks

7.3fit

LOAM

Embodied learningCeilinged
Depth

Real, narrow, honest — and it tops out at the neolithic by construction, with no channel to materials or mechanisms. Its fix is an option layer: parameterised multi-tick construction routines searched by quality-diversity over spatial descriptors, so a channel is one decision rather than four hundred.

Why

The strongest evidence-of-depth artifact anywhere in the pool, and it is a chart: mean yield-gene inside versus outside tribal territory, diverging over months. Domestication here is not a recipe unlocked, it is directional selection on an evolving genome distribution driven by the tribe's own harvesting preference — which is the actual archaeological signature, arises with no clever policy required, and is a real emergent event with a real number attached. Semantic risk is essentially nil: every technology is a physical pattern in a raster with a measurable consequence on yield, water or erosion. Nothing can be drivel. The map-with-hot-diff-overlay is a superb four-second check-in because you see the SHAPE of what they built, and e-ink on the wall refreshed at dawn is the best off-screen ritual proposed. Cheapest GPU story that still uses the GPU sensibly.

Risk

The ladder splits and only the bottom half is safe. Domestication lands; landscape engineering probably does not. Burn→delayed nutrient→plant, or dig a 20-cell channel whose water arrives 300 ticks later, is a sparse-reward long-horizon spatially-extended construction problem where a half-built channel pays strictly negative — the exact shape Craftax exists to demonstrate PPO fails at, and Craftax-MA's own conclusion is that IPPO/MAPPO/PQN all fail its long-horizon credit assignment. So months 2-4 (irrigation, terracing, a map a stranger can read) are at serious risk of never arriving. Also the novelty detector as written is a noisy-TV machine: prediction ERROR on a stochastic raster spikes on rain fronts and its own optimiser, not on the first channel. Use compression PROGRESS on held-out replay, not error.

Pairs with

Apocrypha

7.2fit

Ashcairn

Combinatorial inventionCeilinged
Depth

Lowest authored-content risk of the combinatorial group and the compounding is measured in bits, so you will never wonder whether it is working. But abstraction count plateaus at ~30-80 and depth 3-4 on a world-derived task distribution, well short of the claimed 150-400 at depth 8-12.

Why

Near-twin of Emberlisp with two mechanisms worth stealing outright. First, the transmission bottleneck as a selection pressure on the LIBRARY rather than the tribe: only K functions can be demonstrated per season, so a large library is a liability for a small band and tribes with smaller, better-compressed libraries outcompete hoarders. Watching a civilization refactor its own tech tree for teachability is genuinely counter-intuitive and not authorable. Second, multi-world migration as an EXPERIMENT the system runs on itself nightly: a function that survives transplant into a differently-generated world is the strongest available evidence that the abstraction is real rather than overfit. The audit is right that this should be promoted from validation to training-data generation for the search prior.

Risk

Two errors in the pitch are load-bearing. 'MDL compression IS metabolic efficiency' is a category error — Stitch minimises corpus AST size, not execution cost, and a four-token abstraction can loop 40,000 VM steps, so the compressor and the survival selector will fight. Re-rank Stitch candidates by amortised step savings before admitting them. And the higher-order endgame (`repeat_until_stable(f)`, `teach(f, learner)`, an abstraction that constructs abstractions) is fantasy as described: Stitch finds maximal-compression subtree patterns with term holes; it does not synthesise combinators or metaprograms. Separately, a bigram prior over a growing library gives almost no guidance and will stall at depth 2-3, not 6 — the neural recogniser is the plan, not the fallback, and it is the one job worth giving the idle 5070.

Pairs with

Emberlisp

7fit

Apocrypha

ChroniclesCeilinged
Depth

Bounded but genuinely unauthored within its bounds, and its own one-change points straight at the hybrid: graft a generated chemistry substrate under the ritual DSL so the cultural machinery — hitchhiking, conformist and prestige bias, schism, stemma — has an unbounded supply of real things to sanctify. Smallest change in the pool with the largest ceiling gain.

Why

The cheapest project here and the one most likely to still be delighting at month 12 — and it earns that with a mechanism that is a faithful executable model of a well-supported theory (Henrich's account of adaptive practice accumulating through imitation and differential survival with nobody understanding why; the manioc-detoxification case). Watching `boil` reach fixation in surviving villages without anyone selecting for it, on Saturday, with no LLM in the loop, is a genuine and moving day-one payoff. The hitchhiking mechanic — a useless step fixes forever because it sat next to a useful one, and the critical apparatus can name the village and generation it entered — is the best single piece of unauthored drama in the pool. The critical edition (variant readings, apparatus, stemma, apocrypha) is a real hour-long binge and the phone card is a single verse with a footnote, which is a lovely ten-second read. Runs on nothing; correctly identified as the project to run ALONGSIDE a heavier one on the same laptop, which is the most practically useful sentence in all thirty documents.

Risk

The adaptive fact set is enumerable in an afternoon — ~14 primitives, half functional, crossed with a handful of hazards gives maybe 20-50 truths, all found within weeks. After that the system cycles rather than discovers, and the open-ended layer (BECAUSE symbols, doctrine) is content-free BY DESIGN, which is an accurate model of one thing about religion and not what the builder asked for. Also note the anti-repetition machinery: an all-time n-gram ban over a small subject matter is a monotonically tightening constraint that stalls or forces strained synonym churn by month 4 — window it to 90 days and let rotating scribes with style cards carry the load.

Pairs with

The Slow Kiln (library-learning lens)

6.6fit

The Long Teaching

Library learningCeilinged
Depth

No independent depth mechanism; it is a transmission and loss layer. But it is the best such layer available, and its fix for writing is historically right as well as computationally possible: give marks an INTRAGENERATIONAL payoff (pay ticks to read a mark and skip part of a re-derivation, now, in this program) so writing arrives by exaptation instead of foresight.

Why

Contributes the two mechanisms I would put in whatever gets built, regardless of which engine wins. (1) Knowledge is embodied: `knows(agent, abstraction, fidelity)`, teaching is a costed program, and when the last carrier dies the abstraction is deleted and every dependent entry recursively invalidated — a dark age with a stack trace. A non-monotonic history genuinely cannot be faked with a tech tree, and 'the Reed clan has forgotten TWIST-CORD (age 611 nights, 9 dependents orphaned)' is a devastating notification that requires no LLM to be moving. (2) The rediscovery detector: a new abstraction whose behaviour signature on a fixed probe set matches a previously-deleted one but whose BODY differs. That is convergent re-invention, computed, and it is the only signal in the entire pool that cannot be fooled by a naming model. Make it the centrepiece, not a bullet.

Risk

Weak as a standalone. Nine tribes on one machine is ~1/9 the node budget each on top of an enumerator that was already the bottleneck, and forgetting actively destroys accumulated depth, so expect per-tribe depth 3-4 and 20-40 live abstractions. Convergence is worse than stated: contact, trade and raid are all homogenising, so nine tribes under one rule set will hold maybe 6-10 genuinely distinct abstractions wearing nine generated names. And the headline rung — writing, via `mark`+`read` composing into a durable decodable encoding — is out-of-horizon, not hard: the payoff accrues to a different agent after the inventor is dead, and a per-night budget searching survival predicates has no credit-assignment path to it. Score the idea on the rungs below rung 5.

Pairs with

Emberlisp

6.2fit

Marginalia

Agent societiesCeilinged
Depth

A transmission simulator with a mediocre invention engine — correctly self-diagnosed. Bolt it onto a compression engine operating on the same DSL and the abstractions get minted by Stitch AND corrupted by copying, which is strictly better than either alone.

Why

The best mutation operator in the pool and the best binge screen, both cheap. Scribal error — dittography, haplography, homoeoteleuton, numeral corruption, and above all marginal-gloss absorption (a copyist's note copied INTO the recipe body, historically the single most productive corruption there is) — is a real, ~120-line, structurally productive variation mechanism that nobody else proposed. Copy_of_id gives you an exact stemma for free, so the manuscript family tree with version diffs is genuinely an hour you lose without meaning to. The historian's chronicle written ONLY from surviving texts, then diffable against ground truth, is the best writing constraint in all thirty documents and costs nothing.

Risk

No invention engine at all — it borrows one, and its own technology curve is flat by week 4-8 because line-level edits to ASTs have no smooth genotype-phenotype map and the beneficial mutations you do get are overwhelmingly deletions of redundant steps (efficiency, not capability). LLM interpretation of damaged text adds prior-biased variation, which beats white noise but regresses toward plausibility, i.e. it is a contracting operator. Institutions are parameterized authorship dressed as emergence: scriptorium is a building with an error-rate modifier, canon is a rich-get-richer rule you wrote, schism is a threshold you chose. Vary the thresholds and confirm the phenomena persist before believing them. And a public write endpoint feeding a corpus you publish is a permanent adversarial moderation obligation, not a cheap classifier call.

Pairs with

Palimpsest (chronicle lens)

All 42

Tap any idea for its full design and the audit that attacked it. Every card ends with the one change that raises its ceiling — often the most useful line on the page.

Combinatorial invention

The tech tree generates itself by combination — the adjacent possible with a mechanism under it

▸

The Slow Kiln

A single continent of foraging tribes where nothing is an "item" — everything is a substance with a typed property vector, and invention is a process verb applied to substances, adjudicated by a local LLM that is only allowed to fill typed slots and is then audited by a mechanical conservation-law referee. The tech tree is the closure of {growing verb set} × {growing substance set} under laws you wrote once, and no one authored a single recipe.

Ceilinged
The world

256×256 hex continent, 6–20 tribes of 8–40 agents. The substrate is SUBSTANCES, not items: every substance is a row `{id, name, emoji, phase, mass_per_unit, hardness, temp_K, energy_density_J, toughness, conductivity, opacity, reactivity, toxicity, workability, tags[]}`. You author ~24 primordials (flint, river clay, oak, birch bark, sinew, fat, ochre, reed, marrow…) and nine process verbs (STRIKE, HEAT, GRIND, SOAK, DRY, MIX, BIND, CUT, FERMENT). Terrain deposits substances; deposits deplete locally; seasons and a ~40-year drought cycle move what is available. Agents have calorie/warmth/injury/childbirth accounting and an inventory. Tags are typed: STATE / STRUCTURE / FUNCTION, and a FUNCTION tag carries an affordance — `FUNCTION:cuts` makes a substance usable as the instrument of CUT; `FUNCTION:seals` UNLOCKS A NEW VERB (SEAL) for whoever holds it. Verb invention is the second-order combinatorics: Arthur's "new domains".

How invention actually happens

An attempt is `(verb, sorted[input substance ids], instrument?, ambient temp/wetness bucket)`. Five-stage adjudication, cheapest first: 1. EXACT CACHE. key = sha1(verb ‖ sorted inputs ‖ ambient bucket ‖ law_vector_hash) → `recipes` table in SQLite (WAL). Hit = zero cost. Negative results are cached too ("nothing happens") and are ~60% of the table. 2. SIGNATURE CACHE (the important one). Bucket each input into a coarse typed signature: (phase, hardness∈{0-2,3-5,6-8,9-10}, temp bucket, top-2 tags). If (verb, signature multiset) has ≥3 prior adjudications that agreed on an outcome class, apply the stored property-delta template deterministically and mint the result with a template-derived name. No model call. This is what makes cost sublinear in |substances|. 3. LOCAL ORACLE. llama.cpp server, Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M, GBNF grammar compiled from the property JSON schema via llama.cpp's json-schema-to-grammar. It cannot invent a free-form outcome: it emits `{outcome: TRANSFORM|COMBINE|NOTHING, props delta, ≤2 new tags each with declared type+affordance, name, 12-word why}`. Grammar makes the syntax free; the schema makes the semantics typed. 4. MECHANICAL REFEREE (pure code, no model). Mass: Σout ≤ Σin. Energy: HEAT can only raise temp_K by fuel energy_density / mass. Monotonicity: GRIND cannot raise hardness; DRY cannot raise a WET tag; nothing may simultaneously gain hardness, gain toughness and lose mass beyond an alloy budget. Tag legality: STRUCTURE tags require a structural verb in the attempt. On violation, one retry with the violated law quoted verbatim in the prompt; second violation → record NOTHING. This is the anti-nonsense mechanism and it runs at ~microseconds. 5. NIGHTLY ESCALATION. Rank the day's new recipes by consequence (Δ in tribe survival metrics attributable to them), send the top ~30 in ONE claude-haiku-4-5 Batch job for a better name, a lore line, and a property audit that can overrule the local model. This is also the chronicle voice.

Why it doesn't bottom out

State is not a list of names; it is a point in a ~14-dimensional continuous property space crossed with the power set of a GROWING typed tag vocabulary, crossed with a growing verb set. Attempts available at any moment ≈ V·N²/2 (verbs × substance pairs), and each success can add a substance and, rarely, a verb — which multiplies the frontier rather than adding to it. Two-level combinatorics (Arthur: technologies are combinations, and new domains are the big jumps) means the reachable set is not enumerable in advance and I cannot tell you its size. Where it actually bottoms out: the tag vocabulary's semantic capacity. A 4B model has a finite number of distinct physical concepts; expect a few thousand genuinely distinct tags before new tags are synonyms of old ones. Also note the honest cache-economics wrinkle: pair space is quadratic, so cache hit rate would collapse as N grows — it doesn't, only because agents recombine what is in hand, which keeps the effective frontier local and small.

The ladder

WEEK 1: 24 primordials, 9 verbs. Within ~2 hours of sim time you get sharp flint, cordage, fire, cured hide — and at least one thing you did not author (HEAT(birch bark) → pitch is the classic first surprise). WEEK 2–4: heat chains. Charcoal raises the achievable temperature, which is a *technological variable*, not a stat you wrote; first ceramic; someone coins an AIRFLOW tag and a bellows-equivalent appears because the tag unlocks a verb. MONTH 2–3: metallurgy without you writing metallurgy — alloys are continuous property blends, so the space of alloys is literally continuous. First machine tool: a substance whose FUNCTION tag unlocks a verb only it enables, so a tool that makes tools becomes a bottleneck object worth killing for. MONTH 4–6: glass and optics, fermentation chains, medicine (toxicity-negating tags), recipes traded as secrets by specialists. WILDEST PLAUSIBLE: a nine-step composite from clay+ash+sinew with a property vector nobody planned (high toughness, low mass) that becomes load-bearing for every surviving tribe — and the chronicle can render its full nine-node ancestry DAG on your phone.

What kills you

Calories, warmth, injury, childbirth, winter. Substances deplete locally, so success creates its own scarcity (kilns exhaust nearby wood, forcing the charcoal invention — niche construction as a designed pressure). Raiding takes goods but not knowledge: a recipe spreads only by demonstration, requiring co-location for K ticks with a holder. When the last holder of a recipe dies, that lineage loses it — Henrich's Tasmania model implemented directly. Writing is a reachable invention that changes the loss rate, so the tribes can invent their own defence against forgetting, and you get to watch which one does it first.

How the world changes

A 12-constant LAW VECTOR (gravity, atmospheric oxygen, water boiling point, ambient temp curve, ore abundance, decay rate…). New worlds are spawned by perturbing it; the law hash is part of every cache key, so results legitimately differ across worlds and the cache never lies about it. Within a world: volcanism and ice ages every ~3000 ticks shift what the ground gives, which strands technologies whose inputs vanished. Worlds can be seeded by migrants from another world, carrying a bottlenecked recipe subset into a place where half of it no longer works.

How it knows something is new

Four independent scorers, each z-scored against a rolling 30-day baseline. (a) FIRST-EVER: canonical event signature absent from an `event_signatures` table. (b) KEYSTONE: the new node is an articulation point in the recipe DAG — removing it disconnects ≥5% of reachable tech (Tarjan, milliseconds). This is the best signal in the design: it detects "a technology everything now depends on". (c) MDL SHOCK: nightly compressed size of the tribe behaviour log drops >2σ (they learned to compress their lives = a real efficiency win). (d) REGIME CHANGE: CUSUM change-point on eight world aggregates (population, calorie surplus, tech depth, trade volume, max achievable temperature…). Push only if max z > 3, hard cap of 3 pushes/day, plus one guaranteed 07:00 digest so the coffee ritual never depends on a threshold firing.

On your phone

One hero card: giant emoji, the invented name, "FIRST EVER · night 41", a five-line ancestry chain as plain text (`flint → sharp flint → …`), a population spark, and one gauge — MAX ACHIEVABLE TEMPERATURE (K) — which is the single number that says how advanced the world is. Tap the card for the full ancestry DAG as inline SVG; tap the gauge for the chronicle. Five seconds tells you whether last night mattered; an hour of tapping through ancestry chains is a genuinely good evening.

Your hardware

llama.cpp server with Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M (~3.4 GB weights) + 8k ctx KV ≈ 5.5 GB — comfortable inside 8 GB with room for bge-small embeddings. `--parallel 4` continuous batching; a grammar-constrained adjudication is ~150 output tokens, so budget 1–2 s each → 2–4k novel adjudications/hour, ~30k overnight, which is far more than a warmed cache needs. The sim is node + SQLite on CPU; world state is under 200 MB, so 64 GB RAM is irrelevant here (that is fine — not every idea needs to use every resource). Cloud: ~200–300 Haiku 4.5 Batch calls/night. Haiku 4.5 is $1/$5 per MTok, Batch is 50% off, cached prompt reads ~0.1×: with a 2k-token cached system prompt that is roughly $0.0005/call → about $0.05–0.15 per night, $2–5/month. Do not run QLoRA here; there is nothing to finetune.

What you have to do

Zero required. Optional god-tier nudge: edit `worlds.yaml` to change a law constant, or hit a Tailscale endpoint from your phone to inject an event (meteor, a stranger arriving with a gift substance, a plague). Ten seconds, high leverage, entirely skippable.

First weekend

SATURDAY: the property schema, 24 primordials, 9 verbs, the SQLite tables, the referee laws, and one grammar-constrained llama.cpp call. Build a CLI where YOU combine things and watch the referee reject the model's free lunches. SUNDAY: 12 dumb agents with hunger and an attempt policy (weight attempts by inventory × novelty), the recipe DAG, and a static HTML page listing FIRSTS. The delight moment lands Saturday night, before any agents exist: your conservation referee catches the model conjuring mass, quotes the law back at it, and the retry produces something better than you would have written.

Six months in

An archipelago of law-vector variants; ~1,500–4,000 substances; 25–60 verbs; tribes with visibly divergent materials cultures; recipes as tradeable, stealable, losable secrets; an ancestry DAG that no longer fits on a screen. By then you are reading the chronicle for the sociology (who hoards, who teaches, who forgot) as much as for the chemistry.

Where it stops (self-reported)

The oracle is a plausibility engine, not physics — depth here is depth of narrative-and-property consistency, not truth, and a materials scientist would laugh at month four's metallurgy. Expect tag saturation at month 5–8: new tags start being synonyms, keystone events get rare, and the DAG grows wide instead of deep. New law vectors and harsher needs buy months, not forever. Two concrete failure modes to plan for: (1) if you let agents attempt arbitrary pairs, the quadratic pair space destroys your cache hit rate and your token bill; inventory locality is load-bearing, not flavour. (2) The 4B model writes boring names; without the nightly Haiku pass the chronicle is unreadable within two weeks.

Stack

node + better-sqlite3 for the sim core and the whole world DB (WAL mode, one file per world — trivially backed up and diffed). Python only for the llama.cpp client and nightly analytics. llama.cpp with GBNF from json-schema-to-grammar. Anthropic Python SDK Batch API for the nightly chronicle. PWA: static build exported nightly to GitHub Pages, live build served over Tailscale. ntfy.sh topic for push (their PWA + Web Push means no app store, no Firebase). SHARED SUBSTRATE with ideas 5 and 6: the typed-substance schema, the referee, the lineage/event DB and the PWA chronicle are the same code.

Verified

Verified by search: Infinite Craft's mechanic is exactly cache-then-LLM — every pair is checked against a database of prior combinations, only true novelties hit the model, and results are cached so all players see the same outcome (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinite_Craft; https://www.conjecture.dev/research/building-an-infinite-craft-clone-with-tactics). Arthur O'Dwyer's analysis models the result as a directed hypergraph where each edge maps 1–2 vertices to exactly one vertex (https://quuxplusone.github.io/blog/2024/03/03/infinite-craft-theory/), and reports the scale problem honestly (>1M unique items, >10M recipes). Working open clones to crib architecture from: https://github.com/bufferhead-code/opencraft, https://github.com/ngruychev/finite-craft (explicitly runs on a laptop without a good GPU), https://github.com/CharlesFauman/infini-craft. Grammar-constrained decoding is production-ready in llama.cpp: GBNF plus a json-schema-to-grammar converter covering types, enums, required, additionalProperties, oneOf/anyOf (https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/grammars/README.md) — and the docs make the key point that the schema constrains output but is NOT injected into the prompt, so you must describe the structure in the prompt too. Model sizing verified: Qwen3.5-4B Q4 is ~3.4 GB and is the standard pick for 8 GB machines; Qwen3.5-9B Q4_K_M at ~6.6 GB also fits 8 GB if you want a smarter oracle at lower throughput (https://benchlm.ai/best/local-llm, https://www.mayhemcode.com/2026/06/best-local-llms-for-4gb-6gb-and-8gb.html). Pricing checked against the current Anthropic model table: claude-haiku-4-5 $1/$5 per MTok, 200K ctx; Batch API 50%; prompt-cache reads ~0.1× and writes ~1.25×. Theory: W. Brian Arthur, *The Nature of Technology* (2009) for combination-and-domains; Kauffman's adjacent possible. Tasmania/knowledge-loss model: Henrich 2004 (https://www2.psych.ubc.ca/~henrich/Website/Papers/HenrichTasmania.pdf). Push: ntfy.sh is a PWA with Web Push and self-hostable (https://docs.ntfy.sh/releases/).

The auditCeilinged
Where it really stops

Two independent walls, both closer than the doc's 'month 5–8 tag saturation'. (a) VERB GROWTH IS NOMINAL. The whole second-order claim rests on FUNCTION:seals unlocking SEAL. But nothing in the referee knows what SEAL *does* — the referee only checks mass/energy/hardness monotonicity, which are verb-agnostic. A new verb is a new string in a prompt. Arthur's 'new domains' means new *mechanism*; here it means a new noun. So the multiplicative term in V·N²/2 never actually multiplies anything structural, and the frontier grows quadratically, not exponentially. (b) SIGNATURE CACHE EATS THE ORACLE. Stage 2 fires after 3 agreeing adjudications on a coarse bucket (phase, 4 hardness bins, temp bin, top-2 tags) — a few hundred buckets total. By week 3–4 essentially every attempt hits a stored property-delta template, and the system becomes a deterministic 14-D vector blender that mints interpolants with template names. 'Alloys are a continuous space' is not depth, it is a continuum of near-duplicates. Realistic: genuine surprise through ~week 3; recognisable-but-derivative through ~week 8; after that the DAG grows wide, never deep. Not 6 months.

Is the discovery real?

HIGH — the most important thing to say about this design. HEAT(birch bark) → pitch is presented as 'the classic first surprise'. It is not a surprise; it is retrieval. A 4B model has read the internet and holds a compressed copy of human material culture. The tech tree here is not authored by the designer — it is authored by pretraining, and the sim walks it in an inventory-constrained random order. That is strictly worse than a hand-authored tree in one respect: a hand-authored tree is at least consistent. The designer's own cost argument proves the point — 'agents recombine what is in hand, which keeps the effective frontier local and small' — a small local frontier over a prior-supplied answer key is exactly a tech tree being revealed. You cannot simultaneously claim the space is vast and that locality saves your cache; locality is what makes it affordable AND what makes it shallow. Genuinely non-authored content does exist here (continuous property trajectories the model never saw), but it is the least interesting layer and the LLM re-names it back into familiar categories.

Compute honesty

HONEST — the strongest of the six on this axis, and the cost model is basically right. Corrections: 150 grammar-constrained output tokens on a 4B Q4 at realistic 5070 decode is ~3–4s single-stream, not 1–2s; GBNF adds measurable per-token overhead on a large schema grammar (the doc budgets none). 2–4k novel adjudications/hour needs --parallel 4 saturated and is a peak, not an average; 30k/night assumes 8h at peak. Haiku arithmetic checks out (~$0.0006/call, ~$0.20/night). The 8GB fit for Qwen3-class 4B Q4 + 8k KV is real. The 9B fallback claim is NOT: 6.6GB weights + KV + CUDA context on an 8GB card OOMs or thrashes.

Nonsense risk

MEDIUM-HIGH, and the referee does not address it. The referee catches conservation violations. Almost all nonsense in this system is *categorical*, not thermodynamic: GRIND(sinew, fat) → 'conductive resin, FUNCTION:seals' violates no conservation law and is pure drivel. The five listed laws (mass, energy, GRIND-hardness monotonicity, DRY/WET, tag legality) are a thin sieve over a very large space of plausible-sounding garbage. The nightly Haiku audit is the real anti-nonsense mechanism, and it only sees the top ~30 by consequence.

Claims to re-check

'Qwen3.5-4B/9B' — I cannot verify a Qwen3.5 release; Qwen3-4B is what I can confirm. Cited as verification: benchlm.ai and a mayhemcode.com post dated 2026/06 — content-farm sources presented at the same evidentiary weight as papers. '~60% of the table is negative results' is invented precision. Arthur O'Dwyer's hypergraph post is a personal blog cited alongside peer-reviewed work without distinction (the analysis is fine; the framing isn't). The llama.cpp GBNF / json-schema-to-grammar claims are correct, including the good catch that the schema is not injected into the prompt. Infinite Craft caching description is correct. Henrich 2004 is cited across four of these six ideas as settled; it is contested (Vaesen et al. 2016 PNAS; Read's critiques) and the population-size→complexity link does not replicate cleanly — building four designs on it as a load-bearing 'result' is a shared weakness.

The one change that raises the ceiling

Make verbs mechanical instead of lexical. Require every invented verb to be *defined* as a small program over the referee's own property algebra (a composition of existing operators with bound parameters, synthesised and validated against observed transformations), not just named by the LLM. Then a new verb genuinely enlarges the reachable set, tag→verb unlocking becomes real second-order combinatorics rather than vocabulary growth, and the signature cache can no longer flatten everything because new verbs create new signature spaces. This is the one edit that turns the design from 'Infinite Craft with conservation laws' into something with a real second level.

▸

Ashcairn

Tribes whose every behaviour is a program in a tiny survival DSL, and whose entire technology is a library of named lambda abstractions that a compressor carves out of yesterday's successes each night. The technologies are literally the abstractions — they compound forever because each one shortens the description of everything built on it, and calories are the search budget, so compression is metabolism.

Ceilinged7.2
The world

A deterministic 96×96 tile world with an honest small physics: heat diffusion, water, plant growth, wear, load. Tribes of 6–30 agents. The twist: an agent's behaviour is a PROGRAM in a stack DSL called SURV — about 40 primitives (`move`, `sense`, `take`, `put`, `hit`, `heat`, `wait`, `count`, `if`, `loop`, `nearest`, `carry_with`…). There is no scripted AI. What a tribe *knows* is a library of named lambda abstractions over SURV: `make_cordage`, `bank_the_fire`, `winter_cache`, `two_person_carry`. Objects exist, but they are inert until some program in the library knows what to do with them — knowledge is the primary asset and it is a data structure you can print.

How invention actually happens

DreamCoder's wake-sleep loop, with Stitch as the compressor, embedded in a survival economy. WAKE (all day, CPU, deterministic VM with a STEP BUDGET, never wall-clock): the world poses tasks (hungry, cold, this broke, the river moved). An agent solves by (i) retrieving library functions by embedding similarity on the task description (bge-small, 130 MB), (ii) enumerative/beam search over the library guided by a learned prior — start with a bigram over primitives, graduate to a 2-layer MLP; (iii) when search fails N times, and only then, the local 4B proposes a program SKETCH with holes that search fills. That is LILO's neurosymbolic split: LLM reaches concepts enumeration can't, symbolic compression consolidates them. SLEEP-ABSTRACTION (nightly, ~10 min): take every program that succeeded today across all tribes and run **Stitch** (`stitch_core`, Rust with Python bindings) to find the abstractions that maximally compress the corpus. Any abstraction with compression gain above a bit threshold becomes a NEW NAMED PRIMITIVE with an inventor, a date, and a lineage. LILO's AutoDoc step: the local model writes its name and one-line docstring. That named primitive IS a discovered technology — it is in the library, it is inheritable, it is teachable, it is losable. SLEEP-DREAMING: replay library functions against randomised worlds to train the search prior. This is the step that makes tomorrow's search reach deeper than today's, and it is why the thing compounds instead of plateauing at depth 2.

Why it doesn't bottom out

Programs are unbounded, and the alphabet grows. Each new abstraction shortens the description length of a family of behaviours, which brings previously-unreachable behaviours inside the same search budget — that is the compounding DreamCoder actually demonstrated (it rediscovered classic list routines and physical laws from bare primitives). The space is not the bound; the bound is search budget × task-distribution richness, and the world generates the tasks, so as long as the world keeps changing the task distribution never freezes. This is the only engine here where the depth metric is a real number: abstraction depth in the library DAG, and bits saved.

The ladder

WEEK 1: 40 primitives grow to ~55. Abstractions like "walk to nearest X and take it" — trivial, but they were carved out, not written. WEEK 2–4: `keep_fire_alive`, `two_person_carry`; then the real milestone, the first abstraction whose body contains two other invented abstractions (depth 2). MONTH 2: depth 4–5 abstractions that read like practices — crop rotation, kiln tending, a division-of-labour protocol parameterised by role. MONTH 3–6: HIGHER-ORDER abstractions that take other abstractions as arguments — `repeat_until_stable(f)`, `teach(f, learner)`, `try_then_fallback(f,g)`. The moment a tribe invents a *method* rather than a technique is the moment this stops feeling like a toy. Tool-making programs whose output is an object whose use requires yet another program. WILDEST PLAUSIBLE: the library contains an abstraction that constructs abstractions — an invented apprenticeship protocol — and you can point at the exact night it appeared and the three ancestors it came from.

What kills you

VM step budget = calories. A behaviour costing 4000 steps eats more than one costing 900, so MDL compression IS metabolic efficiency — the single most elegant coupling in this document, and it means the compressor's objective and the survival objective are the same objective. Winter culls tribes with thin caloric margin. Libraries pass to children through a TRANSMISSION BOTTLENECK: only K functions can be demonstrated per season, so a large library is a liability for a small tribe (Henrich's Tasmania result falls straight out). Tribes with smaller, better-compressed libraries outcompete hoarders — which is a genuinely counter-intuitive dynamic you will watch happen.

How the world changes

World-generation parameters mutate each era: rainfall, predator pressure, ore placement, a new organism with new affordances. Because the sim is CPU-cheap, run 3–5 worlds in parallel with different generators and let libraries migrate via a wanderer who can carry at most 5 functions across. A function that survives transplant into a differently-generated world is the strongest possible evidence that the abstraction is real rather than overfit — that is a genuine experiment your system runs on itself, nightly.

How it knows something is new

MDL-native, so almost no false positives — the signal is a number of bits. Push when tonight's Stitch run produces an abstraction that is (a) in the top 1% of all-time compression gain, (b) arity ≥2 with a body containing ≥2 previously-invented abstractions (a composition-depth record), or (c) the first-ever higher-order abstraction. Second detector: a held-out task suite, auto-generated and never trained on, evaluated nightly — push when a task CLASS flips from 0% to >50% solved, which is a capability frontier crossing rather than a lucky run. Third: a LOSS alarm when an abstraction's speaker count hits zero.

On your phone

A library card. Tonight's new named function, its AutoDoc line, the bits saved, its three ancestors, and the pretty-printed body (5–8 lines of SURV — short by construction, because that's what compression means). Two sparklines: LIBRARY DEPTH and CALORIES PER SOLVED TASK (falling = they are getting good at being alive). Tap for the library DAG; tap a function to watch its program execute in the browser, because the VM is compiled to WASM and the PWA can replay any program from any night.

Your hardware

This is the honest CPU idea, and that is a feature: Stitch is Rust and fast, the SURV VM does millions of steps/sec, and 64 GB RAM lets you hold every program corpus from every night in memory for retro-compression experiments. GPU is used for three optional things: the 4B sketch proposer when search stalls, bge-small embeddings for retrieval, and — if you want the Ouroboros Forge flywheel inside a world — a weekly QLoRA on the sketch model using ONLY verified programs. Unsloth supports Blackwell/RTX 50-series (needs CUDA 12.8), and a 4B QLoRA fits 8 GB, but you must stop the inference server first and expect an hour of the laptop running hot. BENCHMARK HYGIENE, non-negotiable: fitness is VM step counts and material counts, never seconds — a thermally-throttling laptop would otherwise silently reshape evolution overnight and you would never find the bug.

What you have to do

None. Optional weekly: read the auto-generated UNSOLVED TASKS list over coffee and hand-write one task if something obvious is missing. This is the one idea where a five-minute weekly nudge measurably changes the trajectory, because the task distribution is the real bottleneck.

First weekend

SATURDAY: the SURV VM, 40 primitives, six hardcoded tasks, plain enumerative search with a depth cap. SUNDAY: pip install stitch_core, feed it the day's successful programs, print the top abstraction with its bit gain, add it to the library, re-run. The delight moment is unmistakable and arrives Sunday afternoon: the compressor names a subroutine you never wrote, and then Monday's search *uses* it to solve something it couldn't reach yesterday.

Six months in

300–800 named abstractions, depth 8–12, several tribes with divergent libraries, and a lineage view where the interesting fact is that a two-line function from night 12 sits underneath 60% of everything they now do. You will find yourself reading SURV like a language.

Where it stops (self-reported)

The hard cap is your primitive set and your world's task richness: if the 40 primitives cannot express chemistry, no amount of compression will invent chemistry. Compression gains flatten around 150–400 abstractions unless world complexity keeps growing — and growing it is the one real maintenance burden in this idea, so be honest with yourself about that. Enumerative search over a growing library also gets slower; the learned prior has to actually work, and a bigram prior will likely stall around depth 6, at which point you are writing a neural recogniser and this becomes a real ML project. DreamCoder's published results used serious compute; a laptop version will be slower and shallower. But the compounding is real, visible within 48 hours, and measured in bits — you will never have to wonder whether it is working.

Stack

Python for the wake-sleep orchestrator; `stitch_core` from PyPI for compression (POPL'23 algorithm, 3–4 orders of magnitude faster than DreamCoder's compressor); the SURV VM written once in Rust and compiled to both native (headless speed) and WASM (in-browser replay for the PWA); SQLite for library, lineage and corpus; llama.cpp + Qwen3.5-4B for sketches and AutoDoc; ntfy.sh push; PWA over Tailscale plus a nightly static export to GitHub Pages. SHARED SUBSTRATE with idea 4: the archive/lineage DB, the chronicle PWA and the push layer are the same.

Verified

Verified: DreamCoder (Ellis et al., PLDI 2021) — iterative wake-sleep alternating between searching for solutions and refactoring shared abstractions into a library that guides future search (https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3453483.3454080). Stitch (Bowers et al., POPL 2023) is the practical compressor: corpus-guided top-down synthesis, 3–4 orders of magnitude faster and 2 orders less memory than the prior compressor at equal-or-better compressivity, open source with Python AND Rust bindings (https://github.com/mlb2251/stitch, https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.16605, crate `stitch_core` at https://lib.rs/crates/stitch_core). LILO (ICLR 2024) is the neurosymbolic version I am copying: STITCH compression + LLM-generated AutoDoc producing an interpretable library of λ-abstractions, beating a language-guided DreamCoder on all three domains, with the explicit finding that LLM-guided search finds concepts enumerative search cannot while compression consolidates them into reusable abstractions (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.19791). Voyager for the skill-library-plus-auto-curriculum shape and its concrete numbers: skill library of executable code indexed by embeddings of natural-language descriptions, iterative prompting with environment feedback + execution errors + a self-verification call, 63 unique items over 160 iterations, 3.3× prior SOTA (https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291, https://voyager.minedojo.org/). Successors exist if you want more ideas: Optimus-1's hierarchical knowledge graph memory, XENON's use of failed experiences to revise dependency knowledge. Unsloth on Blackwell/RTX 50-series is confirmed supported and needs CUDA 12.8; QLoRA on 8 GB handles up to ~11B (https://unsloth.ai/docs/blog/fine-tuning-llms-with-blackwell-rtx-50-series-and-unsloth). Tasmania/transmission-bottleneck loss: Henrich 2004 (https://www2.psych.ubc.ca/~henrich/Website/Papers/HenrichTasmania.pdf).

The auditCeilinged
Where it really stops

The task distribution, not the compressor — and the doc half-admits it in `input_cadence`. Stitch will happily compress whatever you feed it; a 96×96 survival world with hunger, cold, breakage and a moving river generates maybe 8–15 genuinely distinct behaviour families. Compression gain flattens when the corpus stops containing new recurring structure, and that happens at roughly 30–80 abstractions and composition depth 3–4, not '150–400' and not depth 8–12. Timeline: real compounding visible in 48h (true, and the best first-weekend payoff of the six), depth-2 abstractions week 2, depth 3 around week 4–6, then a long flat stretch where nightly Stitch runs return sub-threshold gains and you start hand-writing tasks to keep it moving. DreamCoder's published depth came from *staged, curated* task curricula across hundreds of tasks per domain; 'the world poses tasks' is a much thinner and much less compositionally structured curriculum than list-processing or LOGO.

Is the discovery real?

LOWEST OF THE SIX. Abstractions are genuinely carved out of behaviour by a compressor, with an inventor, a date, a bit-gain, and a printable body. Nothing about `make_cordage` was written by the designer. This is the only idea where 'discovery' survives the merciless reading. The one leak is the LLM sketch proposer: when enumeration stalls and the 4B proposes a program sketch, the LLM's prior about survival strategy enters the library — but it enters as *holes filled by verified search against a deterministic VM*, so it is checked, not asserted. That is the right containment.

Compute honesty

HONEST — genuinely a laptop project, and correct about which parts are which. Stitch is Rust and fast at this corpus size; the VM does millions of steps/sec; 64GB for retro-compression is a real use. Two corrections. (1) 'A bigram prior will stall around depth 6' is optimistic by a factor of two or more — a bigram over primitives gives almost no guidance over a growing library and will stall at depth 2–3; you will be writing the neural recogniser in month one, and the doc should treat that as the plan, not the fallback. (2) The nightly training signal for that recogniser is thin: DreamCoder's dreaming phase worked because replay generated large synthetic corpora; with one small world you will overfit the prior to the current world's tasks, which is the exact opposite of what you need. The multi-world migration test is a good instinct and should be promoted from validation to training-data generation. QLoRA-on-8GB is a distraction here and the doc is right to make it optional; 'up to ~11B on 8GB' is marketing, not a usable configuration.

Nonsense risk

NEAR ZERO — the best of the six. Programs run or they don't; the VM is the adjudicator and it cannot be sweet-talked. Nonsense here manifests as *uninteresting* abstractions (a compressed pattern with no meaning a human would name), not as false ones, and the AutoDoc naming pass makes them look better than they are — which is a mild honesty risk in the chronicle, not in the mechanism.

Claims to re-check

TWO REAL ERRORS. (1) 'MDL compression IS metabolic efficiency — the single most elegant coupling in this document.' This is a category error and it is load-bearing. Stitch minimises description length of the *corpus* (program size in AST nodes), not execution cost. A four-token abstraction can loop 40,000 VM steps. Compression and calories are not the same objective and will diverge immediately; Stitch will invent short, expensive abstractions and your survival selection will punish them, so the two halves of the engine fight. Fix by re-ranking Stitch's candidate abstractions by amortised step savings across the corpus before admitting them to the library. (2) 'Higher-order abstractions that take other abstractions as arguments — `repeat_until_stable(f)`, `teach(f, learner)` … an abstraction that constructs abstractions.' Stitch finds maximal-compression subtree patterns with *term* holes. It does not synthesise higher-order combinators, and it certainly does not produce metaprograms. You would need first-class functions in SURV *and* a corpus that already contains multiple hand-instantiated versions of the pattern before compression could abstract over it — at which point you authored it. The 'invented apprenticeship protocol' wildest-plausible is fantasy as described. Smaller: LILO's ICLR'24 result and Stitch's POPL'23 speedups are stated correctly; Voyager's 63 items / 160 iterations / 3.3× is correct; DreamCoder's physics-law rediscovery is correct but was a symbolic-regression domain, not a situated agent.

The one change that raises the ceiling

Replace world-posed tasks with an OMNI-EPIC-style auto-curriculum: a generator that proposes new tasks conditioned on the current library, gated by learning progress (push tasks just outside the frontier, retire ones solved or never solved). The compressor is not the bottleneck and never was — the task distribution is, and the design already concedes this by asking the human for one hand-written task a week. Automating that single loop is the difference between plateauing at depth 4 in month two and continuing to compound.

▸

The Closure

A world made of reactions rather than things, where a settlement is alive only if its known reactions form a self-sustaining autocatalytic set closed over what the land freely gives — computed exactly, every tick, by the polynomial RAF algorithm. Invention is the discovery of a catalyst; the dramatic event is a CLOSURE, the moment a loop becomes self-sustaining, and its mirror is a cascade collapse when one catalyst goes extinct.

Ceilinged
The world

Six to twenty settlements on a geography that decides the FOOD SET F — the stuffs the land gives freely (sun, water, clay, reed, game, a seam of ore). Everything else is a STUFF produced by a REACTION `inputs → outputs, catalysed by C`. Materials, foods, tools and even practices are all stuffs of different types, treated uniformly. A settlement's knowledge is a set of reactions it has discovered. Its actual working technology is its maxRAF: the largest subset that is Reflexively Autocatalytic and Food-generated — every reaction catalysed by something the set produces or that is in F, every reactant reachable from F. Everything outside the maxRAF is knowledge they hold but cannot run: recipes for a machine they cannot build. That distinction — knowing versus being able — is the whole emotional core.

How invention actually happens

Two coupled generators plus one exact algorithm. (1) GENERATIVE REACTION GRAMMAR. Reactions are never enumerated up front. Typed rewrite operators (JOIN, SPLIT, SUBSTITUTE, REFINE, CATALYSE) generate candidate reactions from the *current* stuff set, so the candidate space grows with every discovery. (2) LAZY CATALYSIS ORACLE. Whether stuff X catalyses reaction r is decided on demand: first by cheap typed compatibility rules (a hard hot thing catalyses a shaping reaction on a soft warm thing), and only for consequential cases by the local 4B under a GBNF grammar emitting `{catalyses: bool, efficiency 0..1, one-line why}`. Cached forever, keyed by (stuff signature, reaction signature, world chemistry seed). Because catalysis is the only thing the model decides, the token bill is tiny and the model's arbitrariness is confined to one relation. (3) THE RAF FIXPOINT (Hordijk & Steel). Roughly fifty lines: repeatedly delete any reaction lacking a producible catalyst or a producible reactant until nothing changes; what remains is the maxRAF. Polynomial, runs per settlement per tick without noticing. Run it on the flow graph too (a simple relaxation or small LP) to get throughput. INVENTION = an agent tries a candidate reaction; success adds it to the known set. The event worth caring about is when adding one reaction makes the maxRAF grow DISCONTINUOUSLY — twelve dormant reactions suddenly become runnable because the missing catalyst arrived. That is a closure, it is exactly computable, and it is the most satisfying thing a graph can do.

Why it doesn't bottom out

The reaction grammar generates candidates from the current stuff set, so each new stuff multiplies candidates, and catalysis is a relation over stuff × reactions — quadratic in a growing set. Kauffman's core result is the reason this has structure rather than just size: as catalysis density crosses a threshold, RAFs appear as a phase transition, and every closure raises the local density, seeding the next transition in a new region. So progress is punctuated, not linear, which is precisely the "and then one day something wild" texture the builder asked for. Honest bound: the operator set of the grammar. With JOIN/SPLIT/SUBSTITUTE alone, stuffs are effectively strings over an alphabet — infinite, but structurally repetitive after a while. Adding an operator is the god-tier nudge that reopens the space.

The ladder

WEEK 1: the hearth closure. Fire is catalysed by dry tinder, which is produced by drying, which is catalysed by fire. Your first self-sustaining cycle, drawn as a highlighted loop, and it will genuinely delight you because you did not put it there — the grammar found it. WEEK 2–4: a pottery closure; then COUPLING, where one closure's waste is another's food, and the two become one bigger RAF. MONTH 2: parasitic sub-RAFs. Model ritual and story as reactions too: a practice that consumes surplus and catalyses cohesion is a real RAF member, so religion emerges as metabolism and can be analysed the same way. MONTH 3–6: CLOSURE TRADE — two settlements each holding half of a RAF, neither able to run it alone. That is the mechanical birth of interdependence, and therefore of politics, treaties and hostage-taking. WILDEST PLAUSIBLE: a RAF spanning three settlements that survives the loss of any one of them — a genuinely distributed, robust industry — and the inverse, a single catalyst's extinction cascading through 40% of the network in one night, rendered as an animation you will watch more than once.

What kills you

A settlement dies when its RAF cannot produce maintenance stuffs (calories, warmth, replacement tools) at the required rate; throughput comes from the flow relaxation on the RAF, so bigger and denser RAFs mean surplus, which means more people, which means more attempts per day and faster invention. That endogenous demographic feedback loop is the growth engine, and it runs in reverse just as well. Catalysts have HOLDERS (people, places, objects) — so the Tasmania effect is structural, not bolted on: a small settlement mathematically cannot hold a large RAF, because too many catalysts have a single point of failure.

How the world changes

F is geography, so geography is physics here. Move the coastline, exhaust the ore seam, shift the climate and the same reaction knowledge yields a different maxRAF — knowledge that was technology becomes folklore. New worlds get a different F and a different CHEMISTRY SEED that biases the catalysis prior, so the same grammar builds a different periodic table. Migration is the cruelest and best mechanic in the design: a band carries its reaction knowledge to a new place and the RAF simply does not close there, and the maths tells you exactly which one missing catalyst is the reason.

How it knows something is new

Closure events are the detector — this design gets its novelty signal for free from exact graph computation, with essentially zero false positives. Push on: the first-ever closure of a new sub-RAF; the largest single-night jump in maxRAF size; a FRAGILITY ALARM (a catalyst whose removal would collapse ≥30% of the RAF is now held by ≤2 people — a min-cut computation, and a genuinely suspenseful notification); and any collapse of >20%. Once closures become routine at month three, switch the statistic from "a closure happened" to "closure rate deviates >3σ" — the detector has to age with the world, and you should plan that from the start.

On your phone

One picture: the maxRAF as a small circular force-free graph laid out by ring depth, with tonight's new cycle glowing. Three numbers: RAF SIZE, THROUGHPUT, FRAGILITY (min-cut). One headline: "NIGHT 63 — charcoal closed the iron loop; 14 dormant reactions joined the working set." A glance tells you whether the civilization got bigger, faster, or more brittle. Tapping a node shows who holds that catalyst and what dies without them.

Your hardware

The lightest of the six. RAF fixpoint is polynomial and tiny at these sizes; candidate pools of millions of reactions fit comfortably in 64 GB RAM (this is the one idea where the RAM matters more than the GPU). The GPU runs the local 4B for catalysis adjudication, batched nightly rather than in the inner loop — a few thousand calls a night at ~200 tokens each is under an hour of GPU time. 8 GB VRAM is a complete non-issue. You could run this on a Raspberry Pi if you dropped the oracle.

What you have to do

Zero. Optional: hand-place a resource, or add one operator to the reaction grammar when things feel repetitive — that is the single highest-leverage twenty-minute intervention in the design.

First weekend

SATURDAY: implement the RAF fixpoint against 30 hand-written reactions and a food set, and prove it finds the hearth loop and correctly refuses a loop with no external catalyst. This is a satisfying, fully-testable Saturday with a real algorithm at the end of it. SUNDAY: the generative grammar, the lazy catalysis oracle, and one settlement that tries things while you watch. Delight moment: the highlighted cycle appearing on its own, from reactions the grammar generated rather than ones you typed.

Six months in

An economy where you can point at the exact reaction whose loss would end a civilization; settlements at war over a single catalyst; a browsable atlas of every closure event with its date and discoverer; and a phone view that is a living metabolic map of a society.

Where it stops (self-reported)

This is only as rich as the reaction grammar — the grammar is the game, and if it is boring the world is boring no matter how elegant the RAF maths. Catalysis assigned by an LLM inherits its arbitrariness; assigned by typed rules it inherits their finiteness; there is no third option and you will oscillate between them. Expect this to feel like an *economy and infrastructure* simulator rather than a cavemen-discovering-fire story unless you invest properly in the narrator — the maths is beautiful but it is not, by itself, a story. And closures stop being surprising around month three, so the novelty detector must be re-based onto rates, which is a real (if small) piece of maintenance.

Stack

Python or node + SQLite. The RAF algorithm hand-rolled (~100 lines, a fixpoint loop; there is no need for a library and writing it yourself is the fun part). Reaction grammar as typed rewrite rules in a small module. llama.cpp GBNF for catalysis. Hand-rolled SVG graph rendering in the PWA — no chart library, the layout is a ring by production depth. ntfy.sh push, Tailscale-served PWA plus nightly GitHub Pages export.

Verified

Verified: RAF sets are Hordijk & Steel's formalisation of Kauffman's collectively autocatalytic sets — 'reflexively autocatalytic and food-generated' — with a polynomial-time algorithm that detects whether a RAF exists in a general network of interacting entities, implemented and applied to Kauffman's original binary-polymer model (original paper: 'Detecting autocatalytic, self-sustaining sets in chemical reaction systems', J. Theor. Biol. 2004 — https://www.math.canterbury.ac.nz/~m.steel/Non_UC/files/research/autocatalytic-edited.pdf; structural theory and algorithms review: https://royalsocietypublishing.org/doi/10.1098/rsif.2018.0808; tractable models: https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.03953; formal framework slides: https://members.elsi.jp/~sympo/1st_20130327/14_hordijk/src/28_3_10_hordijk.pdf). The 'general RAF setting is NP-hard but graph-theoretic restrictions give efficient polynomial algorithms' framing is from the Royal Society Interface review and is exactly the tradeoff you want. Artificial-chemistry neighbours worth reading before you commit to a grammar: AlChemy (Fontana & Buss, untyped lambda calculus) with a 2025 ALIFE paper on steering it via endogenous features that synthesises Church addition from simple primitives (https://direct.mit.edu/isal/proceedings/isal2025/37/28/134038); Combinatory Chemistry (Kruszewski & Mikolov) for combinator-based emergent evolution; Sayama's Hash Chemistry as a minimalist open-ended system (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.18027, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.12790); Buliga's chemlambda, a Turing-complete artificial chemistry with purely local reversible rewrites. Also relevant and encouraging: 'An Open-Ended Approach to Understanding Local, Emergent Conservation Laws in Biological Evolution' (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.03345).

The auditCeilinged
Where it really stops

Understand what RAF actually is: a *detector*, not a generator. The maxRAF fixpoint invents nothing — it reports which subset of an already-generated network is self-sustaining. So every claim about depth reduces to a claim about the five-operator rewrite grammar plus the catalysis oracle, and the elegant polynomial algorithm is downstream decoration. The grammar's honest description is in the doc itself: 'with JOIN/SPLIT/SUBSTITUTE alone, stuffs are effectively strings over an alphabet — infinite, but structurally repetitive.' That is the whole ceiling in one sentence. You will have 10^5 stuffs that are concatenations of clay/reed/ochre with no differentiating structure, and the RAF over them will be large and meaningless. Second and sharper wall: the design has one knob (catalysis density) and a narrow window. Kauffman's result is that RAFs appear *as a phase transition* in catalysis probability. Below the window nothing ever closes and the world is dead; above it, everything closes at once and closure stops being an event by week 2 — the doc says 'closures stop being surprising around month three'; I'd say week 3. You will spend the project hand-tuning the catalysis prior to stay in a band, which is designer intervention wearing a mathematics costume. Realistic: the hearth closure lands weekend 1 and it will genuinely delight; coupled closures by week 3; then an economy simulator that grows in size but not in kind.

Is the discovery real?

MEDIUM. The RAF layer is real, exact, and unauthored — closures genuinely are discovered, and 'which single catalyst's loss collapses 40% of the network' is a real computed fact, not a narrative.
